Well, doctor, I guess I came in because... well, things at the store are getting complicated. I supervise the retail team, and lately, when I try to explain the inventory numbers or lay out the weekend schedule for everybody, my words just start jumping around. Like, I'll be talking about the delivery trucks, and then suddenly I'm on about Thursdays. 

Thursday is usually when we prep the church grill, actually. I've been manning that grill for a few years now, ever since we got that new smoker. It uses hickory wood, gives the brisket this unbelievable flavor, you really have to watch the temperature though or it dries out... but anyway, then suddenly I'll be talking about my boss’s tone of voice, because he's been sounding real sharp lately, and my team... they just look at me. No, I don't think so. If there's evidence for something, I'll believe it. I'm a practical guy, you know? It's like... well, it's like when customers come up to the service desk trying to return an item without a receipt. They'll swear up and down they just bought it on Tuesday, but I can look right at our system and see we haven't even stocked that brand of power drill since last Christmas. 

We switched over to a new hardware supplier, actually. The new guys are based out of Ohio, and let me tell you, their shipping times are just atrocious. We waited three weeks for a pallet of outdoor extension cords right before the holiday rush. My brother-in-law lives in Ohio, actually. Up near Cleveland. He's always complaining about the lake-effect snow. I keep telling him he should move down here where it's warmer, but he's got this stubborn streak. He's been trying to restore this old pontoon boat in his driveway for the better part of a decade. His neighbors absolutely hate it. The homeowner's association keeps sending him these certified letters, but he just throws them right in the trash... 

But, uh, wait, what did you ask me? Something about evidence? Right, yeah, no. If someone actually shows me proof that I'm wrong about something, I just accept it. I'm not stubborn like Dave.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I feel like you just asked me that, didn't you? But no, like I was saying, I don't have anything like that. If you show me the proof, I believe it. 

It's kind of like corporate, you know? They always want proof of our shrink numbers. They send these auditors down from Chicago twice a year to check the books. The last guy they sent was this kid, barely out of college, wearing this ridiculous tailored suit to a home goods store. He spent three hours trying to reconcile the garden center fertilizer pallets. I had to tell him, look, bags break. Customers drop them in the parking lot, we sweep it up, it goes in the compactor. You can't track every single ounce of Miracle-Gro. 

I used to use that stuff on my tomatoes, actually. I had a really fantastic garden back in the early 2000s before the deer figured out how to jump my back fence. They ate everything right down to the stems. I tried putting up chicken wire, spraying that garlic and pepper spray stuff they sell, but those deer, they just don't care. They'll look right at you while they chew up your prize beefsteaks. My neighbor tried to tell me to get a dog to scare them off, but I work fifty hours a week, I don't have time to walk a dog... 

But, uh, anyway. No, I don't have beliefs like that. Like I said, if there's clear proof of something, I just accept it.

They stand there and look at me like they can’t follow a single thing I’m saying. It's like I'm speaking a different language to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts feel jumbled or disorganized, like one idea doesn’t logically follow the next, even when you’re not under stress or distracted?\n\nPatient: Well, when I'm not stressed... I don't really think they're jumbled. I mean, when I'm just at home, things are pretty quiet. I like to sit in my recliner and watch those history documentaries on PBS. There was this fascinating one about the transcontinental railroad just the other night. They had to blast through solid granite in the mountains using just black powder at first. Can you imagine the danger in doing something like that? The workers dealt with these terrible, freezing winters up there. I’ve always hated the snow, honestly. We had a blizzard back in '93 that shut down the whole retail plaza for a week, we lost a ton of perishable inventory... but, uh, anyway. \n\nWhat was I... oh, right. I tell them to rotate the dairy, first in, first out, but it's like it goes right out the other ear. We had a whole pallet of yogurt expire last month because they just shoved the new boxes right in front of the old ones. The new guys are based out of Ohio, and let me tell you, their shipping times are just atrocious. We waited three weeks for a pallet of outdoor extension cords right before the holiday rush. My brother-in-law lives in Ohio, actually. Up near Cleveland. He's always complaining about the lake-effect snow. I keep telling him he should move down here where it's warmer, but he's got this stubborn streak. He's been trying to restore this old pontoon boat in his driveway for the better part of a decade. Are you asking the same thing again? Because no, not really. I don't have any weird beliefs that people don't understand. I'm pretty grounded, honestly. I just stick to the reality of things, you know? 

It's like... well, it's like these new planograms corporate sends down every quarter. They don't want to lug a forty-pound bag of calcium chloride past the decorative pillows and the area rugs. I’ve been putting in maintenance tickets for a replacement battery since October, but the regional facilities guy just ignores my emails. He’s always at these leadership retreats down in Orlando or somewhere, posting pictures on the company portal while we're up here dealing with a broken scrubber and a mountain of crushed rock salt... 

But, uh... wait. Did I answer your question? I don't have any strong beliefs that aren't true, no. Just normal stuff.

They don't want to lug a forty-pound bag of calcium chloride past the decorative pillows and the area rugs. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Look, Doc, I feel like we're just going in circles here. Are you reading off a checklist or something? Because I keep telling you, no. I don't have any made-up beliefs. Everything I deal with is based on real-life situations. 

I mean, you want a real-life situation? Try dealing with the cardboard baler at my store. The hydraulic pump on that thing gave out right in the middle of a double-truck delivery day last month. You have any idea how much cardboard comes off two full trailers of seasonal merchandise? We had broken-down boxes stacked up to the ceiling in the receiving bay. Total fire hazard. I told the store manager, I said, if the fire marshal walks in here right now, we are getting shut down. But he wouldn't approve the emergency repair fee. Said we had to wait for the regular maintenance guy who only comes on Thursdays. 

Thursdays! And this was a Monday. So I had my guys hauling stacks of cardboard out to the overflow bins behind the strip mall, which technically we're not supposed to do because the property management company fines us if we use the shared dumpsters. The lady who runs the leasing office, she's got it out for us anyway ever since one of our cart pushers accidentally dinged her Lexus. It wasn't even his fault, really, the wind caught the carts, but she came storming into the store demanding we pay for a whole new paint job...

But yeah, real life. That's all I focus on. I don't have time to sit around making up things that aren't true.

I don't... Doc, seriously, are you okay? Because this is the exact same question. Just worded differently. It's like you're trying to trip me up or something, but the answer is still no. I don't have experiences like that. I don't believe things that aren't happening.

I told him, Gary, the drivers don't even have access to the breakroom, they just drop the pallets and leave. But he wouldn't let it go. He started bringing his own padlock for his lunchbox. 

And speaking of lunches, the new vendor they got for the deli across the street? Total garbage. Used to be you could get a decent pastrami on rye for six bucks. Now it's twelve dollars for something that tastes like shoe leather, and they skimp on the mustard. I went in there last Tuesday, asked for extra mustard, and the kid behind the counter looked at me like I asked him for his firstborn child. I just wanted a sandwich, you know? I only get thirty minutes for lunch on a good day, and usually I end up eating it standing up by the customer service desk anyway because somebody always needs an override on a return...

So, yeah. No. I don't have those experiences. I'm just dealing with reality. Can we move on to something else now?

I don't really think they're jumbled. I mean, when I'm just at home, things are pretty quiet. I like to sit in my recliner and watch those history documentaries on PBS. There was this fascinating one about the transcontinental railroad just the other night. They had to blast through solid granite in the mountains using just black powder at first. Can you imagine the danger in doing something like that? The workers dealt with these terrible, freezing winters up there. I’ve always hated the snow, honestly. We had a blizzard back in '93 that shut down the whole retail plaza for a week, we lost a ton of perishable inventory... but, uh, anyway. \n\nWhat was I... oh, right. No, I don't really notice my thoughts getting mixed up when I'm just sitting around relaxing or watching TV. I'm not entirely sure I know what you mean by that, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not your own, or like someone is putting thoughts into your head?\n\nPatient: No... no, I don't feel like that. My thoughts are my own. I mean, sometimes I wish I could put thoughts into other people's heads, you know? Like my stockroom guys. I tell them to rotate the dairy, first in, first out, but it's like it goes right out the other ear. We had a whole pallet of yogurt expire last month because they just shoved the new boxes right in front of the old ones. The distributor wouldn't even give us a credit for it, which really messed up my margins for the quarter. Those delivery guys are always in such a rush anyway, they just drop the pallets and run. One of them backed his truck right into the loading dock bumper a few weeks ago and took a massive chunk out of the concrete. We had to get a contractor out to patch it before the safety inspector came by... but, uh, anyway. \n\nNo, nobody is putting thoughts into my head. It's just my own stuff up there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't, even after they've shown you evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. If there's evidence for something, I'll believe it. I'm a practical guy, you know? It's like... well, it's like when customers come up to the service desk trying to return an item without a receipt. They'll swear up and down they just bought it on Tuesday, but I can look right at our system and see we haven't even stocked that brand of power drill since last Christmas. \n\nWe switched over to a new hardware supplier, actually. The new guys are based out of Ohio, and let me tell you, their shipping times are just atrocious. We waited three weeks for a pallet of outdoor extension cords right before the holiday rush. My brother-in-law lives in Ohio, actually. Up near Cleveland. He's always complaining about the lake-effect snow. I keep telling him he should move down here where it's warmer, but he's got this stubborn streak. He's been trying to restore this old pontoon boat in his driveway for the better part of a decade. His neighbors absolutely hate it. The homeowner's association keeps sending him these certified letters, but he just throws them right in the trash... \n\nBut, uh, wait, what did you ask me? Something about evidence? Right, yeah, no. If someone actually shows me proof that I'm wrong about something, I just accept it. I'm not stubborn like Dave.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something was happening or was true, even when everyone around you said it wasn't and showed you clear proof otherwise?\n\nPatient: I feel like you just asked me that, didn't you? But no, like I was saying, I don't have anything like that. If you show me the proof, I believe it. \n\nIt's kind of like corporate, you know? They always want proof of our shrink numbers. They send these auditors down from Chicago twice a year to check the books. The last guy they sent was this kid, barely out of college, wearing this ridiculous tailored suit to a home goods store. He spent three hours trying to reconcile the garden center fertilizer pallets. I had to tell him, look, bags break. Customers drop them in the parking lot, we sweep it up, it goes in the compactor. You can't track every single ounce of Miracle-Gro. \n\nI used to use that stuff on my tomatoes, actually. I had a really fantastic garden back in the early 2000s before the deer figured out how to jump my back fence. They ate everything right down to the stems. I tried putting up chicken wire, spraying that garlic and pepper spray stuff they sell, but those deer, they just don't care. They'll look right at you while they chew up your prize beefsteaks. My neighbor tried to tell me to get a dog to scare them off, but I work fifty hours a week, I don't have time to walk a dog... \n\nBut, uh, anyway. No, I don't have beliefs like that. Like I said, if there's clear proof of something, I just accept it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that wasn’t true and that other people couldn’t understand, even if it didn’t involve them showing you proof?\n\nPatient: Are you asking the same thing again? Because no, not really. I don't have any weird beliefs that people don't understand. I'm pretty grounded, honestly. I just stick to the reality of things, you know? \n\nIt's like... well, it's like these new planograms corporate sends down every quarter. They strongly believe—and I mean, they will not hear otherwise—that moving the seasonal aisle to the very back of the store is going to increase impulse buys in the home goods section. I told my district manager, I said, look, when people come in for rock salt in January, they want it right by the front doors. They don't want to lug a forty-pound bag of calcium chloride past the decorative pillows and the area rugs. We actually had a guy last winter drop a whole bag right in the middle of the main action alley. Salt everywhere. \n\nAnd our industrial floor scrubber, the battery on that thing is completely shot. You plug it in all night, and it still dies halfway down aisle four. I’ve been putting in maintenance tickets for a replacement battery since October, but the regional facilities guy just ignores my emails. He’s always at these leadership retreats down in Orlando or somewhere, posting pictures on the company portal while we're up here dealing with a broken scrubber and a mountain of crushed rock salt... \n\nBut, uh... wait. Did I answer your question? I don't have any strong beliefs that aren't true, no. Just normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that was completely untrue, that other people couldn’t understand, and that wasn’t based on any real-life event or situation?\n\nPatient: Look, Doc, I feel like we're just going in circles here. Are you reading off a checklist or something? Because I keep telling you, no. I don't have any made-up beliefs. Everything I deal with is based on real-life situations. \n\nI mean, you want a real-life situation? Try dealing with the cardboard baler at my store. The hydraulic pump on that thing gave out right in the middle of a double-truck delivery day last month. You have any idea how much cardboard comes off two full trailers of seasonal merchandise? We had broken-down boxes stacked up to the ceiling in the receiving bay. Total fire hazard. I told the store manager, I said, if the fire marshal walks in here right now, we are getting shut down. But he wouldn't approve the emergency repair fee. Said we had to wait for the regular maintenance guy who only comes on Thursdays. \n\nThursdays! And this was a Monday. So I had my guys hauling stacks of cardboard out to the overflow bins behind the strip mall, which technically we're not supposed to do because the property management company fines us if we use the shared dumpsters. The lady who runs the leasing office, she's got it out for us anyway ever since one of our cart pushers accidentally dinged her Lexus. It wasn't even his fault, really, the wind caught the carts, but she came storming into the store demanding we pay for a whole new paint job...\n\nBut yeah, real life. That's all I focus on. I don't have time to sit around making up things that aren't true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an experience where you believed something was happening to you or around you that wasn’t based on anything that was actually going on at the time—something others couldn’t relate to because it wasn’t connected to real events?\n\nPatient: I don't... Doc, seriously, are you okay? Because this is the exact same question. Just worded differently. It's like you're trying to trip me up or something, but the answer is still no. I don't have experiences like that. I don't believe things that aren't happening. \n\nIt reminds me of this guy we had working in the warehouse, Gary. Gary was always convinced the delivery drivers were stealing the good snacks out of the breakroom vending machine when they came in through the back. I told him, Gary, the drivers don't even have access to the breakroom, they just drop the pallets and leave. But he wouldn't let it go. He started bringing his own padlock for his lunchbox. \n\nAnd speaking of lunches, the new vendor they got for the deli across the street? Total garbage. No, I haven't. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there. I mean, my hearing is perfectly fine. Actually, I just had it checked last year when they made us do those mandatory occupational health screenings. 

The company brought in this mobile clinic thing, parked it right out by the garden center. Took up three prime parking spots on a Saturday, which is our busiest day. I told the regional manager, I said, you can't park a 40-foot RV in the customer lot on a weekend, people are going to complain. And they did. I had a lady yelling at me because she had to park all the way by the Applebee's just to come buy a garden hose. 

And those hoses, by the way, the new brand we're stocking? Absolute garbage. They kink if you even look at them funny. We've had so many returns on them, it's messing up my department's shrinkage numbers. The manufacturer says they're "kink-resistant," but I've got a pile of them in the back room that says otherwise. I keep telling corporate we need to go back to the old supplier, but they signed some exclusive contract to save a few pennies... 

So no, my hearing and vision are fine. I don't see or hear anything unusual. Just the normal chaos of the store.

I tell them to rotate the dairy, first in, first out, but it's like it goes right out the other ear. We had a whole pallet of yogurt expire last month because they just shoved the new boxes right in front of the old ones. The distributor wouldn't even give us a credit for it, which really messed up my margins for the quarter. Those delivery guys are always in such a rush anyway, they just drop the pallets and run. One of them backed his truck right into the loading dock bumper a few weeks ago and took a massive chunk out of the concrete. We had to get a contractor out to patch it before the safety inspector came by... but, uh, anyway. \n\nNo, nobody is putting thoughts into my head. It's just my own stuff up there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't, even after they've shown you evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. If there's evidence for something, I'll believe it. I'm a practical guy, you know? It's like... well, it's like when customers come up to the service desk trying to return an item without a receipt. They'll swear up and down they just bought it on Tuesday, but I can look right at our system and see we haven't even stocked that brand of power drill since last Christmas. \n\nWe switched over to a new hardware supplier, actually. The new guys are based out of Ohio, and let me tell you, their shipping times are just atrocious. We waited three weeks for a pallet of outdoor extension cords right before the holiday rush. My brother-in-law lives in Ohio, actually. Up near Cleveland. He's always complaining about the lake-effect snow. I keep telling him he should move down here where it's warmer, but he's got this stubborn streak. He's been trying to restore this old pontoon boat in his driveway for the better part of a decade. His neighbors absolutely hate it. The homeowner's association keeps sending him these certified letters, but he just throws them right in the trash... \n\nBut, uh, wait, what did you ask me? Something about evidence? Right, yeah, no. If someone actually shows me proof that I'm wrong about something, I just accept it. I'm not stubborn like Dave.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something was happening or was true, even when everyone around you said it wasn't and showed you clear proof otherwise?\n\nPatient: I feel like you just asked me that, didn't you? But no, like I was saying, I don't have anything like that. If you show me the proof, I believe it. \n\nIt's kind of like corporate, you know? They always want proof of our shrink numbers. They send these auditors down from Chicago twice a year to check the books. The last guy they sent was this kid, barely out of college, wearing this ridiculous tailored suit to a home goods store. He spent three hours trying to reconcile the garden center fertilizer pallets. I had to tell him, look, bags break. Customers drop them in the parking lot, we sweep it up, it goes in the compactor. You can't track every single ounce of Miracle-Gro. \n\nI used to use that stuff on my tomatoes, actually. I had a really fantastic garden back in the early 2000s before the deer figured out how to jump my back fence. They ate everything right down to the stems. I tried putting up chicken wire, spraying that garlic and pepper spray stuff they sell, but those deer, they just don't care. They'll look right at you while they chew up your prize beefsteaks. My neighbor tried to tell me to get a dog to scare them off, but I work fifty hours a week, I don't have time to walk a dog... \n\nBut, uh, anyway. No, I don't have beliefs like that. Like I said, if there's clear proof of something, I just accept it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that wasn’t true and that other people couldn’t understand, even if it didn’t involve them showing you proof?\n\nPatient: Are you asking the same thing again? Because no, not really. I don't have any weird beliefs that people don't understand. I'm pretty grounded, honestly. I just stick to the reality of things, you know? \n\nIt's like... well, it's like these new planograms corporate sends down every quarter. They strongly believe—and I mean, they will not hear otherwise—that moving the seasonal aisle to the very back of the store is going to increase impulse buys in the home goods section. I told my district manager, I said, look, when people come in for rock salt in January, they want it right by the front doors. They don't want to lug a forty-pound bag of calcium chloride past the decorative pillows and the area rugs. We actually had a guy last winter drop a whole bag right in the middle of the main action alley. Salt everywhere. \n\nAnd our industrial floor scrubber, the battery on that thing is completely shot. You plug it in all night, and it still dies halfway down aisle four. I’ve been putting in maintenance tickets for a replacement battery since October, but the regional facilities guy just ignores my emails. He’s always at these leadership retreats down in Orlando or somewhere, posting pictures on the company portal while we're up here dealing with a broken scrubber and a mountain of crushed rock salt... \n\nBut, uh... wait. Did I answer your question? I don't have any strong beliefs that aren't true, no. Just normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that was completely untrue, that other people couldn’t understand, and that wasn’t based on any real-life event or situation?\n\nPatient: Look, Doc, I feel like we're just going in circles here. Are you reading off a checklist or something? Because I keep telling you, no. I don't have any made-up beliefs. Everything I deal with is based on real-life situations. \n\nI mean, you want a real-life situation? Try dealing with the cardboard baler at my store. The hydraulic pump on that thing gave out right in the middle of a double-truck delivery day last month. You have any idea how much cardboard comes off two full trailers of seasonal merchandise? We had broken-down boxes stacked up to the ceiling in the receiving bay. Total fire hazard. I told the store manager, I said, if the fire marshal walks in here right now, we are getting shut down. But he wouldn't approve the emergency repair fee. Said we had to wait for the regular maintenance guy who only comes on Thursdays. \n\nThursdays! And this was a Monday. So I had my guys hauling stacks of cardboard out to the overflow bins behind the strip mall, which technically we're not supposed to do because the property management company fines us if we use the shared dumpsters. The lady who runs the leasing office, she's got it out for us anyway ever since one of our cart pushers accidentally dinged her Lexus. It wasn't even his fault, really, the wind caught the carts, but she came storming into the store demanding we pay for a whole new paint job...\n\nBut yeah, real life. That's all I focus on. I don't have time to sit around making up things that aren't true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an experience where you believed something was happening to you or around you that wasn’t based on anything that was actually going on at the time—something others couldn’t relate to because it wasn’t connected to real events?\n\nPatient: I don't... Doc, seriously, are you okay? Because this is the exact same question. Just worded differently. It's like you're trying to trip me up or something, but the answer is still no. I don't have experiences like that. I don't believe things that aren't happening. \n\nIt reminds me of this guy we had working in the warehouse, Gary. Gary was always convinced the delivery drivers were stealing the good snacks out of the breakroom vending machine when they came in through the back. I told him, Gary, the drivers don't even have access to the breakroom, they just drop the pallets and leave. But he wouldn't let it go. He started bringing his own padlock for his lunchbox. \n\nAnd speaking of lunches, the new vendor they got for the deli across the street? Total garbage. Used to be you could get a decent pastrami on rye for six bucks. Now it's twelve dollars for something that tastes like shoe leather, and they skimp on the mustard. I went in there last Tuesday, asked for extra mustard, and the kid behind the counter looked at me like I asked him for his firstborn child. I just wanted a sandwich, you know? I only get thirty minutes for lunch on a good day, and usually I end up eating it standing up by the customer service desk anyway because somebody always needs an override on a return...\n\nSo, yeah. No. I don't have those experiences. I'm just dealing with reality. Can we move on to something else now?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or felt something that others around you didn’t seem to notice—like hearing voices or seeing things that weren’t there?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there. I mean, my hearing is perfectly fine. Actually, I just had it checked last year when they made us do those mandatory occupational health screenings. \n\nThe company brought in this mobile clinic thing, parked it right out by the garden center. Took up three prime parking spots on a Saturday, which is our busiest day. I told the regional manager, I said, you can't park a 40-foot RV in the customer lot on a weekend, people are going to complain. And they did. I had a lady yelling at me because she had to park all the way by the Applebee's just to come buy a garden hose. \n\nAnd those hoses, by the way, the new brand we're stocking? Absolute garbage. They kink if you even look at them funny. We've had so many returns on them, it's messing up my department's shrinkage numbers. The manufacturer says they're \"kink-resistant,\" but I've got a pile of them in the back room that says otherwise. I keep telling corporate we need to go back to the old supplier, but they signed some exclusive contract to save a few pennies... \n\nSo no, my hearing and vision are fine. I don't see or hear anything unusual. Just the normal chaos of the store.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I haven't. I don't get hung up on stuff like that. If there's no evidence for something, why would I waste my time on it? I need facts, you know? Like numbers on a spreadsheet. 

Speaking of spreadsheets, they just updated our inventory management software at work, and let me tell you, it is a complete disaster. The old system, sure, it looked like it was from 1995, but it worked. You scanned a barcode, it told you how many units were in the back, end of story. This new thing? It's all cloud-based and it's supposed to use some kind of algorithm to predict when we're going to run out of stock. But it doesn't account for local trends. Like, it ordered us three pallets of snow shovels. In April. I live in a place where it barely snows in January, let alone April! 

I tried calling the IT helpdesk about it, but you know how that goes. You sit on hold for forty-five minutes listening to elevator music, and then you get somebody reading off a script who tells you to just restart the computer. I told the guy, buddy, restarting the computer isn't going to make three pallets of snow shovels magically turn into the patio furniture we actually need right now. We've got the big spring clearance event coming up, and I've got half the garden center taken up by winter gear...

But anyway, no. I don't believe things without evidence. I stick to what's right in front of me.

I had a really fantastic garden back in the early 2000s before the deer figured out how to jump my back fence. They ate everything right down to the stems. I tried putting up chicken wire, spraying that garlic and pepper spray stuff they sell, but those deer, they just don't care. They'll look right at you while they chew up your prize beefsteaks. My neighbor tried to tell me to get a dog to scare them off, but I work fifty hours a week, I don't have time to walk a dog... \n\nBut, uh, anyway. No, I don't have beliefs like that. Like I said, if there's clear proof of something, I just accept it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that wasn’t true and that other people couldn’t understand, even if it didn’t involve them showing you proof?\n\nPatient: Are you asking the same thing again? They strongly believe—and I mean, they will not hear otherwise—that moving the seasonal aisle to the very back of the store is going to increase impulse buys in the home goods section. Everything I deal with is based on real-life situations. \n\nI mean, you want a real-life situation? Try dealing with the cardboard baler at my store. The hydraulic pump on that thing gave out right in the middle of a double-truck delivery day last month. You have any idea how much cardboard comes off two full trailers of seasonal merchandise? We had broken-down boxes stacked up to the ceiling in the receiving bay. Total fire hazard. I told the store manager, I said, if the fire marshal walks in here right now, we are getting shut down. But he wouldn't approve the emergency repair fee. Said we had to wait for the regular maintenance guy who only comes on Thursdays. \n\nThursdays! And this was a Monday. So I had my guys hauling stacks of cardboard out to the overflow bins behind the strip mall, which technically we're not supposed to do because the property management company fines us if we use the shared dumpsters. The lady who runs the leasing office, she's got it out for us anyway ever since one of our cart pushers accidentally dinged her Lexus. It wasn't even his fault, really, the wind caught the carts, but she came storming into the store demanding we pay for a whole new paint job...\n\nBut yeah, real life. That's all I focus on. I don't have time to sit around making up things that aren't true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an experience where you believed something was happening to you or around you that wasn’t based on anything that was actually going on at the time—something others couldn’t relate to because it wasn’t connected to real events?\n\nPatient: I don't... Doc, seriously, are you okay? Because this is the exact same question. Just worded differently. It's like you're trying to trip me up or something, but the answer is still no. I don't have experiences like that. I don't believe things that aren't happening. \n\nIt reminds me of this guy we had working in the warehouse, Gary. Gary was always convinced the delivery drivers were stealing the good snacks out of the breakroom vending machine when they came in through the back. I told him, Gary, the drivers don't even have access to the breakroom, they just drop the pallets and leave. But he wouldn't let it go. He started bringing his own padlock for his lunchbox. \n\nAnd speaking of lunches, the new vendor they got for the deli across the street? Total garbage. Used to be you could get a decent pastrami on rye for six bucks. Now it's twelve dollars for something that tastes like shoe leather, and they skimp on the mustard. I went in there last Tuesday, asked for extra mustard, and the kid behind the counter looked at me like I asked him for his firstborn child. I just wanted a sandwich, you know? Well, I guess... yeah, actually. That's kind of why I agreed to come in here. It's not just at work, but it happens there a lot. Like, I'll be trying to explain the inventory numbers to the team, or going over the weekend schedule, and suddenly my words just start jumping all over the place. I'll go from talking about the delivery trucks to Thursdays, and then somehow I'm talking about the grill at the church picnic, and then my boss's tone of voice... and I look around, and everyone is just staring at me like they have no idea what I'm saying. They can't follow me. 

And speaking of the church grill, they really need to replace that thing. The burners on the left side are completely shot. We had the spring fellowship last month, and I was trying to cook eighty hot dogs on half a grill. It's ridiculous. I told Pastor Dave, we have money in the maintenance fund, we should just go down to the store—my store, I could get him my employee discount, which is 15% off patio and outdoor, not bad—and just buy a new Weber. But he's got this whole thing about "stewardship" and making do with what we have. Which I get, I really do, but there's a difference between stewardship and serving people raw hot dogs. 

I ended up having to boil half of them in the church kitchen, which just isn't the same. A boiled hot dog is basically just a warm bologna tube. You need that char, you know? You need the grill marks. My dad used to say a hot dog isn't done until it splits down the middle. He used to take us to this place by the lake every summer, and they had these foot-longs... 

But yeah, the words just get jumbled up sometimes. People look at me weird.

It uses hickory wood, gives the brisket this unbelievable flavor, you really have to watch the temperature though or it dries out... but anyway, then suddenly I'll be talking about my boss’s tone of voice, because he's been sounding real sharp lately, and my team... they just look at me. They stand there and look at me like they can’t follow a single thing I’m saying. It's like I'm speaking a different language to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts feel jumbled or disorganized, like one idea doesn’t logically follow the next, even when you’re not under stress or distracted?\n\nPatient: Well, when I'm not stressed... I don't really think they're jumbled. I mean, when I'm just at home, things are pretty quiet. I like to sit in my recliner and watch those history documentaries on PBS. There was this fascinating one about the transcontinental railroad just the other night. They had to blast through solid granite in the mountains using just black powder at first. Can you imagine the danger in doing something like that? The workers dealt with these terrible, freezing winters up there. I’ve always hated the snow, honestly. It's just my own stuff up there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't, even after they've shown you evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. If there's evidence for something, I'll believe it. I'm a practical guy, you know? It's like... well, it's like when customers come up to the service desk trying to return an item without a receipt. They'll swear up and down they just bought it on Tuesday, but I can look right at our system and see we haven't even stocked that brand of power drill since last Christmas. \n\nWe switched over to a new hardware supplier, actually. The new guys are based out of Ohio, and let me tell you, their shipping times are just atrocious. We waited three weeks for a pallet of outdoor extension cords right before the holiday rush. My brother-in-law lives in Ohio, actually. Up near Cleveland. He's always complaining about the lake-effect snow. I keep telling him he should move down here where it's warmer, but he's got this stubborn streak. He's been trying to restore this old pontoon boat in his driveway for the better part of a decade. His neighbors absolutely hate it. The homeowner's association keeps sending him these certified letters, but he just throws them right in the trash... \n\nBut, uh, wait, what did you ask me? Something about evidence? Right, yeah, no. If someone actually shows me proof that I'm wrong about something, I just accept it. I'm not stubborn like Dave.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something was happening or was true, even when everyone around you said it wasn't and showed you clear proof otherwise?\n\nPatient: I feel like you just asked me that, didn't you? But no, like I was saying, I don't have anything like that. If you show me the proof, I believe it. \n\nIt's kind of like corporate, you know? They always want proof of our shrink numbers. They send these auditors down from Chicago twice a year to check the books. The last guy they sent was this kid, barely out of college, wearing this ridiculous tailored suit to a home goods store. He spent three hours trying to reconcile the garden center fertilizer pallets. I had to tell him, look, bags break. Customers drop them in the parking lot, we sweep it up, it goes in the compactor. You can't track every single ounce of Miracle-Gro. \n\nI used to use that stuff on my tomatoes, actually. I had a really fantastic garden back in the early 2000s before the deer figured out how to jump my back fence. They ate everything right down to the stems. I tried putting up chicken wire, spraying that garlic and pepper spray stuff they sell, but those deer, they just don't care. They'll look right at you while they chew up your prize beefsteaks. My neighbor tried to tell me to get a dog to scare them off, but I work fifty hours a week, I don't have time to walk a dog... \n\nBut, uh, anyway. No, I don't have beliefs like that. Like I said, if there's clear proof of something, I just accept it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that wasn’t true and that other people couldn’t understand, even if it didn’t involve them showing you proof?\n\nPatient: Are you asking the same thing again? Because no, not really. I don't have any weird beliefs that people don't understand. I'm pretty grounded, honestly. I just stick to the reality of things, you know? \n\nIt's like... well, it's like these new planograms corporate sends down every quarter. They strongly believe—and I mean, they will not hear otherwise—that moving the seasonal aisle to the very back of the store is going to increase impulse buys in the home goods section. I told my district manager, I said, look, when people come in for rock salt in January, they want it right by the front doors. They don't want to lug a forty-pound bag of calcium chloride past the decorative pillows and the area rugs. We actually had a guy last winter drop a whole bag right in the middle of the main action alley. Salt everywhere. \n\nAnd our industrial floor scrubber, the battery on that thing is completely shot. You plug it in all night, and it still dies halfway down aisle four. I’ve been putting in maintenance tickets for a replacement battery since October, but the regional facilities guy just ignores my emails. He’s always at these leadership retreats down in Orlando or somewhere, posting pictures on the company portal while we're up here dealing with a broken scrubber and a mountain of crushed rock salt... \n\nBut, uh... wait. Did I answer your question? I don't have any strong beliefs that aren't true, no. Just normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that was completely untrue, that other people couldn’t understand, and that wasn’t based on any real-life event or situation?\n\nPatient: Look, Doc, I feel like we're just going in circles here. Are you reading off a checklist or something? Because I keep telling you, no. I don't have any made-up beliefs. Everything I deal with is based on real-life situations. \n\nI mean, you want a real-life situation? Try dealing with the cardboard baler at my store. The hydraulic pump on that thing gave out right in the middle of a double-truck delivery day last month. You have any idea how much cardboard comes off two full trailers of seasonal merchandise? We had broken-down boxes stacked up to the ceiling in the receiving bay. Total fire hazard. I told the store manager, I said, if the fire marshal walks in here right now, we are getting shut down. But he wouldn't approve the emergency repair fee. Said we had to wait for the regular maintenance guy who only comes on Thursdays. \n\nThursdays! And this was a Monday. So I had my guys hauling stacks of cardboard out to the overflow bins behind the strip mall, which technically we're not supposed to do because the property management company fines us if we use the shared dumpsters. The lady who runs the leasing office, she's got it out for us anyway ever since one of our cart pushers accidentally dinged her Lexus. It wasn't even his fault, really, the wind caught the carts, but she came storming into the store demanding we pay for a whole new paint job...\n\nBut yeah, real life. That's all I focus on. I don't have time to sit around making up things that aren't true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an experience where you believed something was happening to you or around you that wasn’t based on anything that was actually going on at the time—something others couldn’t relate to because it wasn’t connected to real events?\n\nPatient: I don't... Doc, seriously, are you okay? Because this is the exact same question. Just worded differently. It's like you're trying to trip me up or something, but the answer is still no. I don't have experiences like that. I don't believe things that aren't happening. \n\nIt reminds me of this guy we had working in the warehouse, Gary. Gary was always convinced the delivery drivers were stealing the good snacks out of the breakroom vending machine when they came in through the back. I told him, Gary, the drivers don't even have access to the breakroom, they just drop the pallets and leave. But he wouldn't let it go. He started bringing his own padlock for his lunchbox. \n\nAnd speaking of lunches, the new vendor they got for the deli across the street? Total garbage. Used to be you could get a decent pastrami on rye for six bucks. Now it's twelve dollars for something that tastes like shoe leather, and they skimp on the mustard. I went in there last Tuesday, asked for extra mustard, and the kid behind the counter looked at me like I asked him for his firstborn child. I just wanted a sandwich, you know? I only get thirty minutes for lunch on a good day, and usually I end up eating it standing up by the customer service desk anyway because somebody always needs an override on a return...\n\nSo, yeah. No. I don't have those experiences. I'm just dealing with reality. Can we move on to something else now?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or felt something that others around you didn’t seem to notice—like hearing voices or seeing things that weren’t there?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there. I mean, my hearing is perfectly fine. Actually, I just had it checked last year when they made us do those mandatory occupational health screenings. \n\nThe company brought in this mobile clinic thing, parked it right out by the garden center. Took up three prime parking spots on a Saturday, which is our busiest day. I told the regional manager, I said, you can't park a 40-foot RV in the customer lot on a weekend, people are going to complain. And they did. I had a lady yelling at me because she had to park all the way by the Applebee's just to come buy a garden hose. \n\nAnd those hoses, by the way, the new brand we're stocking? Absolute garbage. They kink if you even look at them funny. We've had so many returns on them, it's messing up my department's shrinkage numbers. The manufacturer says they're \"kink-resistant,\" but I've got a pile of them in the back room that says otherwise. I keep telling corporate we need to go back to the old supplier, but they signed some exclusive contract to save a few pennies... \n\nSo no, my hearing and vision are fine. I don't see or hear anything unusual. Just the normal chaos of the store.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you believed something so strongly that it affected your daily life, even though there was no real evidence to support it?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I don't get hung up on stuff like that. If there's no evidence for something, why would I waste my time on it? I need facts, you know? Like numbers on a spreadsheet. \n\nSpeaking of spreadsheets, they just updated our inventory management software at work, and let me tell you, it is a complete disaster. The old system, sure, it looked like it was from 1995, but it worked. You scanned a barcode, it told you how many units were in the back, end of story. This new thing? It's all cloud-based and it's supposed to use some kind of algorithm to predict when we're going to run out of stock. But it doesn't account for local trends. Like, it ordered us three pallets of snow shovels. In April. I live in a place where it barely snows in January, let alone April! \n\nI tried calling the IT helpdesk about it, but you know how that goes. You sit on hold for forty-five minutes listening to elevator music, and then you get somebody reading off a script who tells you to just restart the computer. I told the guy, buddy, restarting the computer isn't going to make three pallets of snow shovels magically turn into the patio furniture we actually need right now. We've got the big spring clearance event coming up, and I've got half the garden center taken up by winter gear...\n\nBut anyway, no. I don't believe things without evidence. I stick to what's right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not at work and not under stress, do you ever notice that your thoughts are disorganized or don't follow a logical sequence?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... yeah, actually. That's kind of why I agreed to come in here. It's not just at work, but it happens there a lot. Like, I'll be trying to explain the inventory numbers to the team, or going over the weekend schedule, and suddenly my words just start jumping all over the place. I'll go from talking about the delivery trucks to Thursdays, and then somehow I'm talking about the grill at the church picnic, and then my boss's tone of voice... and I look around, and everyone is just staring at me like they have no idea what I'm saying. They can't follow me. \n\nAnd speaking of the church grill, they really need to replace that thing. The burners on the left side are completely shot. We had the spring fellowship last month, and I was trying to cook eighty hot dogs on half a grill. It's ridiculous. I told Pastor Dave, we have money in the maintenance fund, we should just go down to the store—my store, I could get him my employee discount, which is 15% off patio and outdoor, not bad—and just buy a new Weber. But he's got this whole thing about \"stewardship\" and making do with what we have. Which I get, I really do, but there's a difference between stewardship and serving people raw hot dogs. \n\nI ended up having to boil half of them in the church kitchen, which just isn't the same. A boiled hot dog is basically just a warm bologna tube. You need that char, you know? You need the grill marks. My dad used to say a hot dog isn't done until it splits down the middle. He used to take us to this place by the lake every summer, and they had these foot-longs... \n\nBut yeah, the words just get jumbled up sometimes. People look at me weird.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

  "note": "",

No, not really. When I'm just sitting at home, it doesn't happen. I mean, if I'm just watching TV, I'm not really talking to anybody anyway, right? It's just me and the dog. I usually put on the History Channel or something. 

They used to have really good documentaries on there, you know? Like, actual history. The Civil War, World War II, stuff about the Roman Empire. Now it's all just reality shows about pawn shops and guys digging for gold in Alaska. I don't get how they can even call it the History Channel anymore. It's like MTV. MTV used to play music videos, and now it's just teenagers yelling at each other on reality shows. 

My daughter used to watch those shows all the time when she was in high school. I'd come home from work, and she'd be glued to the screen watching these spoiled rich kids having sweet sixteen parties that cost more than my house. It used to drive me crazy. I told her, you know, when I turned sixteen, my dad took me to the DMV to get my license and then bought me a hamburger. That was it. And I was thrilled! I got to drive the family station wagon home. It was this big, ugly green Ford Country Squire with the fake wood paneling on the side. Thing drove like a boat. 

But yeah, when I'm home, my thoughts are fine. I'm just watching TV, so there's no problem.

I told the guy, buddy, restarting the computer isn't going to make three pallets of snow shovels magically turn into the patio furniture we actually need right now. We've got the big spring clearance event coming up, and I've got half the garden center taken up by winter gear...\n\nBut anyway, no. I don't believe things without evidence. I stick to what's right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not at work and not under stress, do you ever notice that your thoughts are disorganized or don't follow a logical sequence?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... yeah, actually. Which I get, I really do, but there's a difference between stewardship and serving people raw hot dogs. \n\nI ended up having to boil half of them in the church kitchen, which just isn't the same. A boiled hot dog is basically just a warm bologna tube. You need that char, you know? You need the grill marks. My dad used to say a hot dog isn't done until it splits down the middle. He used to take us to this place by the lake every summer, and they had these foot-longs... \n\nBut yeah, the words just get jumbled up sometimes. People look at me weird.\n\nDoctor: When you're at home, just relaxing or watching TV, do your thoughts ever feel disorganized or do you catch yourself speaking in a way that doesn't make sense, even when you're not stressed or distracted?\n\nPatient: No, not really. When I'm just sitting at home, it doesn't happen. I mean, if I'm just watching TV, I'm not really talking to anybody anyway, right? It's just me and the dog. I usually put on the History Channel or something. \n\nThey used to have really good documentaries on there, you know? Like, actual history. The Civil War, World War II, stuff about the Roman Empire. I'm just watching TV, so there's no problem.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m Dr. [Your Name]. Can you tell me what’s been going on that brought you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I guess I came in because... well, things at the store are getting complicated. Like, I'll be talking about the delivery trucks, and then suddenly I'm on about Thursdays. \n\nThursday is usually when we prep the church grill, actually. They stand there and look at me like they can’t follow a single thing I’m saying. It's like I'm speaking a different language to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts feel jumbled or disorganized, like one idea doesn’t logically follow the next, even when you’re not under stress or distracted?\n\nPatient: Well, when I'm not stressed... I don't really think they're jumbled. I mean, when I'm just at home, things are pretty quiet. I like to sit in my recliner and watch those history documentaries on PBS. There was this fascinating one about the transcontinental railroad just the other night. They had to blast through solid granite in the mountains using just black powder at first. Can you imagine the danger in doing something like that? The workers dealt with these terrible, freezing winters up there. I’ve always hated the snow, honestly. We had a blizzard back in '93 that shut down the whole retail plaza for a week, we lost a ton of perishable inventory... but, uh, anyway. \n\nWhat was I... oh, right. No, I don't really notice my thoughts getting mixed up when I'm just sitting around relaxing or watching TV. I'm not entirely sure I know what you mean by that, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not your own, or like someone is putting thoughts into your head?\n\nPatient: No... no, I don't feel like that. My thoughts are my own. I mean, sometimes I wish I could put thoughts into other people's heads, you know? Like my stockroom guys. I tell them to rotate the dairy, first in, first out, but it's like it goes right out the other ear. We had a whole pallet of yogurt expire last month because they just shoved the new boxes right in front of the old ones. The distributor wouldn't even give us a credit for it, which really messed up my margins for the quarter. Those delivery guys are always in such a rush anyway, they just drop the pallets and run. One of them backed his truck right into the loading dock bumper a few weeks ago and took a massive chunk out of the concrete. We had to get a contractor out to patch it before the safety inspector came by... but, uh, anyway. \n\nNo, nobody is putting thoughts into my head. It's just my own stuff up there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't, even after they've shown you evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. If there's evidence for something, I'll believe it. I'm a practical guy, you know? It's like... well, it's like when customers come up to the service desk trying to return an item without a receipt. They'll swear up and down they just bought it on Tuesday, but I can look right at our system and see we haven't even stocked that brand of power drill since last Christmas. \n\nWe switched over to a new hardware supplier, actually. The new guys are based out of Ohio, and let me tell you, their shipping times are just atrocious. We waited three weeks for a pallet of outdoor extension cords right before the holiday rush. My brother-in-law lives in Ohio, actually. Up near Cleveland. He's always complaining about the lake-effect snow. I keep telling him he should move down here where it's warmer, but he's got this stubborn streak. He's been trying to restore this old pontoon boat in his driveway for the better part of a decade. His neighbors absolutely hate it. The homeowner's association keeps sending him these certified letters, but he just throws them right in the trash... \n\nBut, uh, wait, what did you ask me? Something about evidence? Right, yeah, no. If someone actually shows me proof that I'm wrong about something, I just accept it. I'm not stubborn like Dave.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something was happening or was true, even when everyone around you said it wasn't and showed you clear proof otherwise?\n\nPatient: I feel like you just asked me that, didn't you? But no, like I was saying, I don't have anything like that. If you show me the proof, I believe it. \n\nIt's kind of like corporate, you know? They always want proof of our shrink numbers. They send these auditors down from Chicago twice a year to check the books. The last guy they sent was this kid, barely out of college, wearing this ridiculous tailored suit to a home goods store. He spent three hours trying to reconcile the garden center fertilizer pallets. I had to tell him, look, bags break. Customers drop them in the parking lot, we sweep it up, it goes in the compactor. You can't track every single ounce of Miracle-Gro. \n\nI used to use that stuff on my tomatoes, actually. I had a really fantastic garden back in the early 2000s before the deer figured out how to jump my back fence. They ate everything right down to the stems. I tried putting up chicken wire, spraying that garlic and pepper spray stuff they sell, but those deer, they just don't care. They'll look right at you while they chew up your prize beefsteaks. My neighbor tried to tell me to get a dog to scare them off, but I work fifty hours a week, I don't have time to walk a dog... \n\nBut, uh, anyway. No, I don't have beliefs like that. Like I said, if there's clear proof of something, I just accept it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that wasn’t true and that other people couldn’t understand, even if it didn’t involve them showing you proof?\n\nPatient: Are you asking the same thing again? Because no, not really. I don't have any weird beliefs that people don't understand. I'm pretty grounded, honestly. I just stick to the reality of things, you know? \n\nIt's like... well, it's like these new planograms corporate sends down every quarter. They strongly believe—and I mean, they will not hear otherwise—that moving the seasonal aisle to the very back of the store is going to increase impulse buys in the home goods section. I told my district manager, I said, look, when people come in for rock salt in January, they want it right by the front doors. They don't want to lug a forty-pound bag of calcium chloride past the decorative pillows and the area rugs. We actually had a guy last winter drop a whole bag right in the middle of the main action alley. Salt everywhere. \n\nAnd our industrial floor scrubber, the battery on that thing is completely shot. You plug it in all night, and it still dies halfway down aisle four. I’ve been putting in maintenance tickets for a replacement battery since October, but the regional facilities guy just ignores my emails. He’s always at these leadership retreats down in Orlando or somewhere, posting pictures on the company portal while we're up here dealing with a broken scrubber and a mountain of crushed rock salt... \n\nBut, uh... wait. Did I answer your question? I don't have any strong beliefs that aren't true, no. Just normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that was completely untrue, that other people couldn’t understand, and that wasn’t based on any real-life event or situation?\n\nPatient: Look, Doc, I feel like we're just going in circles here. Are you reading off a checklist or something? Because I keep telling you, no. I don't have any made-up beliefs. Everything I deal with is based on real-life situations. \n\nI mean, you want a real-life situation? Try dealing with the cardboard baler at my store. The hydraulic pump on that thing gave out right in the middle of a double-truck delivery day last month. You have any idea how much cardboard comes off two full trailers of seasonal merchandise? We had broken-down boxes stacked up to the ceiling in the receiving bay. Total fire hazard. I told the store manager, I said, if the fire marshal walks in here right now, we are getting shut down. But he wouldn't approve the emergency repair fee. Said we had to wait for the regular maintenance guy who only comes on Thursdays. \n\nThursdays! And this was a Monday. So I had my guys hauling stacks of cardboard out to the overflow bins behind the strip mall, which technically we're not supposed to do because the property management company fines us if we use the shared dumpsters. The lady who runs the leasing office, she's got it out for us anyway ever since one of our cart pushers accidentally dinged her Lexus. It wasn't even his fault, really, the wind caught the carts, but she came storming into the store demanding we pay for a whole new paint job...\n\nBut yeah, real life. That's all I focus on. I don't have time to sit around making up things that aren't true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an experience where you believed something was happening to you or around you that wasn’t based on anything that was actually going on at the time—something others couldn’t relate to because it wasn’t connected to real events?\n\nPatient: I don't... Doc, seriously, are you okay? Because this is the exact same question. Just worded differently. It's like you're trying to trip me up or something, but the answer is still no. I don't have experiences like that. I don't believe things that aren't happening. \n\nIt reminds me of this guy we had working in the warehouse, Gary. Gary was always convinced the delivery drivers were stealing the good snacks out of the breakroom vending machine when they came in through the back. I told him, Gary, the drivers don't even have access to the breakroom, they just drop the pallets and leave. But he wouldn't let it go. He started bringing his own padlock for his lunchbox. \n\nAnd speaking of lunches, the new vendor they got for the deli across the street? Total garbage. Used to be you could get a decent pastrami on rye for six bucks. Now it's twelve dollars for something that tastes like shoe leather, and they skimp on the mustard. I went in there last Tuesday, asked for extra mustard, and the kid behind the counter looked at me like I asked him for his firstborn child. I just wanted a sandwich, you know? I only get thirty minutes for lunch on a good day, and usually I end up eating it standing up by the customer service desk anyway because somebody always needs an override on a return...\n\nSo, yeah. No. I don't have those experiences. I'm just dealing with reality. Can we move on to something else now?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or felt something that others around you didn’t seem to notice—like hearing voices or seeing things that weren’t there?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there. I mean, my hearing is perfectly fine. Actually, I just had it checked last year when they made us do those mandatory occupational health screenings. \n\nThe company brought in this mobile clinic thing, parked it right out by the garden center. Took up three prime parking spots on a Saturday, which is our busiest day. I told the regional manager, I said, you can't park a 40-foot RV in the customer lot on a weekend, people are going to complain. And they did. I had a lady yelling at me because she had to park all the way by the Applebee's just to come buy a garden hose. \n\nAnd those hoses, by the way, the new brand we're stocking? Absolute garbage. They kink if you even look at them funny. We've had so many returns on them, it's messing up my department's shrinkage numbers. The manufacturer says they're \"kink-resistant,\" but I've got a pile of them in the back room that says otherwise. I keep telling corporate we need to go back to the old supplier, but they signed some exclusive contract to save a few pennies... \n\nSo no, my hearing and vision are fine. I don't see or hear anything unusual. Just the normal chaos of the store.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you believed something so strongly that it affected your daily life, even though there was no real evidence to support it?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I don't get hung up on stuff like that. If there's no evidence for something, why would I waste my time on it? I need facts, you know? Like numbers on a spreadsheet. \n\nSpeaking of spreadsheets, they just updated our inventory management software at work, and let me tell you, it is a complete disaster. The old system, sure, it looked like it was from 1995, but it worked. You scanned a barcode, it told you how many units were in the back, end of story. This new thing? It's all cloud-based and it's supposed to use some kind of algorithm to predict when we're going to run out of stock. But it doesn't account for local trends. Like, it ordered us three pallets of snow shovels. In April. I live in a place where it barely snows in January, let alone April! \n\nI tried calling the IT helpdesk about it, but you know how that goes. You sit on hold for forty-five minutes listening to elevator music, and then you get somebody reading off a script who tells you to just restart the computer. I told the guy, buddy, restarting the computer isn't going to make three pallets of snow shovels magically turn into the patio furniture we actually need right now. We've got the big spring clearance event coming up, and I've got half the garden center taken up by winter gear...\n\nBut anyway, no. I don't believe things without evidence. I stick to what's right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not at work and not under stress, do you ever notice that your thoughts are disorganized or don't follow a logical sequence?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... yeah, actually. That's kind of why I agreed to come in here. It's not just at work, but it happens there a lot. Like, I'll be trying to explain the inventory numbers to the team, or going over the weekend schedule, and suddenly my words just start jumping all over the place. I'll go from talking about the delivery trucks to Thursdays, and then somehow I'm talking about the grill at the church picnic, and then my boss's tone of voice... and I look around, and everyone is just staring at me like they have no idea what I'm saying. They can't follow me. \n\nAnd speaking of the church grill, they really need to replace that thing. The burners on the left side are completely shot. We had the spring fellowship last month, and I was trying to cook eighty hot dogs on half a grill. It's ridiculous. I told Pastor Dave, we have money in the maintenance fund, we should just go down to the store—my store, I could get him my employee discount, which is 15% off patio and outdoor, not bad—and just buy a new Weber. But he's got this whole thing about \"stewardship\" and making do with what we have. Which I get, I really do, but there's a difference between stewardship and serving people raw hot dogs. \n\nI ended up having to boil half of them in the church kitchen, which just isn't the same. A boiled hot dog is basically just a warm bologna tube. You need that char, you know? You need the grill marks. My dad used to say a hot dog isn't done until it splits down the middle. He used to take us to this place by the lake every summer, and they had these foot-longs... \n\nBut yeah, the words just get jumbled up sometimes. People look at me weird.\n\nDoctor: When you're at home, just relaxing or watching TV, do your thoughts ever feel disorganized or do you catch yourself speaking in a way that doesn't make sense, even when you're not stressed or distracted?\n\nPatient: No, not really. When I'm just sitting at home, it doesn't happen. I mean, if I'm just watching TV, I'm not really talking to anybody anyway, right? It's just me and the dog. I usually put on the History Channel or something. \n\nThey used to have really good documentaries on there, you know? Like, actual history. The Civil War, World War II, stuff about the Roman Empire. Now it's all just reality shows about pawn shops and guys digging for gold in Alaska. I don't get how they can even call it the History Channel anymore. It's like MTV. MTV used to play music videos, and now it's just teenagers yelling at each other on reality shows. \n\nMy daughter used to watch those shows all the time when she was in high school. I'd come home from work, and she'd be glued to the screen watching these spoiled rich kids having sweet sixteen parties that cost more than my house. It used to drive me crazy. I told her, you know, when I turned sixteen, my dad took me to the DMV to get my license and then bought me a hamburger. That was it. And I was thrilled! I got to drive the family station wagon home. It was this big, ugly green Ford Country Squire with the fake wood paneling on the side. Thing drove like a boat. \n\nBut yeah, when I'm home, my thoughts are fine. I'm just watching TV, so there's no problem.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any time when your thoughts or speech felt disorganized or hard to follow, even when you weren’t at work or under stress—like when you’re out with friends, at church, or running errands?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, if I'm out running errands... not really. I don't think so. I just go in, get what I need, and get out. Like at the grocery store. I always go to the same one, the Kroger over on Elm Street. They know me there. But I'll tell you what, they reorganized the aisles last week and it is a nightmare. \n\nI went in for coffee filters and milk, right? Usually, the coffee is in aisle four, right next to the cereal. Makes sense, breakfast stuff. Now, they moved the coffee to aisle nine, next to the baking supplies. Why? Who bakes with coffee? And the filters weren't even with the coffee! They had them on an endcap near the paper towels. I was wandering around for twenty minutes looking for them. \n\nAnd then I ran into Bob—Bob from church, the guy who usually helps me with the ushering on Sundays. He was looking for canned tomatoes, and they moved those too. We were both just standing there in the middle of the store, completely lost. Bob was telling me his son just got a new job out in Denver, doing something with computers. Good kid, but he never liked the cold. I don't know why he'd move to Denver. I visited Colorado once in the nineties, and it snowed in May. May! We were trying to drive up to Pikes Peak and had to turn around. \n\nBut no, I don't think my speech gets jumbled when I'm out doing stuff like that. I just want to get my groceries and go home, you know?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People look at me weird.\n\nDoctor: When you're at home, just relaxing or watching TV, do your thoughts ever feel disorganized or do you catch yourself speaking in a way that doesn't make sense, even when you're not stressed or distracted?\n\nPatient: No, not really. When I'm just sitting at home, it doesn't happen. I mean, if I'm just watching TV, I'm not really talking to anybody anyway, right? It's just me and the dog. I usually put on the History Channel or something. \n\nThey used to have really good documentaries on there, you know? Like, actual history. The Civil War, World War II, stuff about the Roman Empire. Now it's all just reality shows about pawn shops and guys digging for gold in Alaska. I don't get how they can even call it the History Channel anymore. It's like MTV. MTV used to play music videos, and now it's just teenagers yelling at each other on reality shows. \n\nMy daughter used to watch those shows all the time when she was in high school. I'd come home from work, and she'd be glued to the screen watching these spoiled rich kids having sweet sixteen parties that cost more than my house. It used to drive me crazy. I told her, you know, when I turned sixteen, my dad took me to the DMV to get my license and then bought me a hamburger. That was it. And I was thrilled! I got to drive the family station wagon home. It was this big, ugly green Ford Country Squire with the fake wood paneling on the side. Thing drove like a boat. \n\nBut yeah, when I'm home, my thoughts are fine. I'm just watching TV, so there's no problem.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any time when your thoughts or speech felt disorganized or hard to follow, even when you weren’t at work or under stress—like when you’re out with friends, at church, or running errands?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, if I'm out running errands... not really. I don't think so. I just go in, get what I need, and get out. Like at the grocery store. I always go to the same one, the Kroger over on Elm Street. They know me there. But I'll tell you what, they reorganized the aisles last week and it is a nightmare. \n\nI went in for coffee filters and milk, right? Usually, the coffee is in aisle four, right next to the cereal. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. If I'm just sitting quiet or falling asleep, my mind is pretty calm. I just kind of drift off. 

Though, I'll tell you, falling asleep has been a lot easier since I finally got a new mattress. I had my old one for... gosh, it had to be fifteen years. You know they say you're supposed to replace them every eight years? I went down to the mattress store last month, and being a retail supervisor myself, I can spot a hard sell from a mile away. This kid, he couldn't have been more than twenty-two, comes right up to me trying to push this memory foam thing with the cooling gel and the motorized adjustable base. I told him, look, buddy, I just want a firm innerspring. I don't need to feel like I'm sleeping in a space shuttle. 

Speaking of space, did you see they're trying to send people back to the moon? Artemis, I think the program is called. I remember watching the first Apollo landing with my dad. We had this little black and white TV in the living room, and you had to mess with the rabbit ears with tin foil on them just to get the picture to stop rolling. My mom made popcorn on the stove... the real kind, with the oil in the pot, not the microwave stuff. Microwave popcorn always tastes like chemicals anyway, it leaves that film on the roof of your mouth. But yeah, watching that rocket go up... it was really something. We stayed up way past my bedtime. 

But no, when I'm just relaxing like that, my thoughts are fine.
